> 莎士比亚在《哈姆雷特》中写道：“Brevity is the soul of wit”——简洁是机智的灵魂。与说简洁对机智“重要”或“必不可少”相比，soul 更能唤起联想：仅用一个音节，就指向最深处的本质。这句话也在示范自己的主张，因为它本身就很简短。

It was reiterated centuries later in the famous style manual, The Elements of Style, by Strunk and White, where Professor Strunk, in lectures at Cornell that E.B. White recalled and then turned into a book, would say that the first rule of writing is *omit needless words.* Now, again, it's a beautiful example of itself, because there are no needless words in it. And he said that sometimes Professor Strunk would be so adamant, and so insistent that this was almost the only thing you needed to know about writing, that he would just not know what to do except repeat it. And he'd say, omit needless words, omit needless words, omit needless words. And as with brevity is the soul of wit, aside from being an example of itself, it is so true.

> 几个世纪以后，斯特伦克和怀特在著名的《风格的要素》中又重申了这一点。怀特回忆斯特伦克教授在康奈尔大学的课堂，并将其整理成书。教授说，写作的第一条规则是：*删掉不必要的词。* 这同样是一个优美的自我示范，因为它没有任何多余的词。怀特说，教授有时如此坚决，几乎认定写作只需要知道这一条，以至于不知道还能做什么，就反复念：“删掉不必要的词，删掉不必要的词，删掉不必要的词。”和“简洁是机智的灵魂”一样，它不仅以身作则，而且确实正确。

Well, when I have to compress an article to fit into some fixed space, especially if I'm writing for a newspaper or magazine, it's unlike academia where you can blather on with no one telling you to shut up. When you have to write for a paper, they'll say, sorry, 800 words. And if it's 803 words, the editor will chop off three words and you don't get to say what they are and it can often mangle the meaning. So you have to actually learn how to say something in 800 words or however many words it is. But what I find is **often just the exercise of squeezing it into that maximum limit improves the prose as if by magic.** It's like, oh, damn, I have to make this shorter. I thought I had said it perfectly. Then often when you do it, you find, it really has improved in quality having gotten rid of those needless words. Partly it's good. It's good because of mental effort. Namely, every syllable, every word is more cognitive processing by a reader. If you can get the same message in quicker, that's often less work. But it also makes it aesthetically more pleasing. You have to work within the constraints of the English language, the melody, the rhythm, the music of the language. It often forces you into concrete language instead of woolly idioms and cliches.

> 例如，为报刊杂志写文章时，经常得压缩到规定篇幅，这不同于学界，没人阻止你没完没了地说。报纸会说：“抱歉，只能八百词。”如果有八百零三个词，编辑会自己砍掉三个，轮不到你选，而这常常破坏原意。所以你必须学会在八百词，或其他规定字数内，把事情说清楚。我发现，**仅仅把文章压缩到字数上限，就常常像魔法一样改善文笔**。一开始我会想：“该死，还得缩短？我明明已经写得完美了。”但压完后，往往发现删掉那些多余的词，质量确实更高。部分原因是认知负担：每个音节、每个词，都要读者额外处理。更快传达相同信息，通常意味着更省力。同时，它也更美。你必须在英语的约束下工作，顾及语言的旋律、节奏和音乐性，这常常逼你用具体语言，而不是含糊习语和陈词滥调。


### 4.8　正式与非正式：文风为何发生变化

> *来源：如何在 AI 时代写作：史蒂芬·平克谈清晰表达与语言之美。*

Interviewer: At Rockefeller Center, I saw a stone inscription presenting John D. Rockefeller’s ideas about society and the good life. The writing was remarkably good. The Declaration of Independence also contains beautiful lines, such as “We hold these truths to be self-evident, that all men are created equal.” What do you make of older writing? People often find it harder to read and welcome the clarity of modern prose. Yet I sometimes feel that English has lost some poetic beauty and reverence for language itself.

> 主持人：我在洛克菲勒中心看到一块石碑，上面写着约翰·D. 洛克菲勒关于社会与美好生活的理念，文字好得惊人。《独立宣言》也有优美的句子，例如“我们认为这些真理是不言而喻的：人人生而平等。”你怎么看过去的写作？人们常觉得旧文难读，庆幸现代文章更清楚易懂；但我有时也感觉，英语失去了某种诗意之美，以及对语言本身的敬意。

Pinker: You do sometimes feel that. Rockefeller is a good example because he was an oil magnate, not a professional prose stylist. Thomas Edison wrote beautifully about how electricity would liberate women, although he spent his days working with filaments and light bulbs. Herbert Spencer, Oliver Wendell Holmes Sr., and Charles Darwin were also very good writers, though writing was not their main occupation.

> 平克：有时确实会有这种感觉。洛克菲勒是个好例子，因为他是石油大亨，并非职业文体家。托马斯·爱迪生谈电力如何解放女性时，文字同样优美，尽管他整天研究的是灯丝和灯泡。赫伯特·斯宾塞、老奥利弗·温德尔·霍姆斯、查尔斯·达尔文，也都不是以写作为主业，却很擅长写作。

I think one reason is they were trained on the classics, on great works of literature. Probably because for many of them, there wasn't the telephone, radio, let alone texting, social media. The way that you presented yourself was through your prose. Hmm. And so you cultivated it the same way you kind of arranged yourself in a mirror. Right. This is how other people viewed you. But you had this stock. Because also they were educated on the classics, they had many good examples to draw on. But then the third reason is one that came up earlier in our conversation. Namely, since they didn't have the benefit of decades and decades of abstractions and cliches, and they were kind of exploring virgin territory, conveying new ideas, and they couldn't easily reach for a canned cliché. They had to put new ideas into forms that their readers or listeners could understand. And that forced them to draw on visual images and metaphors and vivid expressions and locutions.

> 我认为，一个原因是他们接受的是经典和伟大文学作品的训练。对许多人来说，那时还没有电话和广播，更别说短信、社交媒体。文字是你展示自己的方式，因此你会像照镜子整理仪容一样雕琢它，因为别人就是通过文字认识你。经典教育又给了他们大量可供借鉴的优秀范例。第三个原因，是我们前面谈到的：他们没有几十年积累的抽象词和套话可以借用，正在探索新的领域、传达新思想，无法轻易伸手拿来现成说法，只能把新思想转化为读者或听众能理解的形式。这逼着他们使用视觉图像、隐喻，以及鲜活的表达。

There has also been a process of informalization lasting more than a century. You can see it in clothing: men no longer routinely wear hats, women no longer wear gloves, and we find old photographs of people hiking in ties and long dresses surprising. People once addressed one another as Mr. or Mrs.; now we often use first names. Calling a teacher by a first name was unthinkable in my childhood.

> 另外，一百多年来还存在一个“非正式化”过程。服饰上就看得出来：男人不再习惯戴帽子，女人不再戴手套；看到旧照片里的人打着领带、穿着长裙去远足，我们会觉得惊讶。过去人们以“先生”“太太”相称，如今常常直接叫名字。在我的童年，直呼老师的名字是不可想象的。

The fact that taboo language.. profanities are commonly woven into speech, which, again, would have been, unthinkable a century ago. But there is a process of greater familiarity, of less hierarchy, partly as a byproduct of democratization, the erosion of traditional hierarchies of class and education. There's a kind of romantic ethos where authenticity, spontaneity, are valued as opposed to putting on airs, thinking carefully before you do anything. All of these have led to fancy language being seen as more pompous, stilted, distancing, whereas the cultural value has changed toward spontaneity, intimacy, naturalness, authenticity. So I think many of us, if we had to put the effort into crafting prose the way they may have a century or two centuries ago, we'd feel, oh, people are thinking I'm being too fancy. And they might even perceive you as being too fancy. Right. And as a result, the conversational vernacular, as opposed to the elevated and well-crafted, has come to characterize modern prose.

> 平克：还有禁忌词和脏话，现在常常混在日常语言里，一百年前同样不可想象。整体上，人与人更熟悉，等级感更弱，部分是民主化以及传统阶级、教育等级瓦解的副产品。还有一种浪漫主义气质，重视真实与自发，而不赞成装腔作势、做什么都要深思熟虑。这些变化让华丽语言显得自大、僵硬、疏远，而文化价值转向随性、亲近、自然与真实。因此，今天如果像一两个世纪前那样费力雕琢文章，很多人会担心：“别人会不会觉得我太讲究了？”别人可能也真会这么想。结果，现代文章的典型风格成了日常口语，而不是高雅、精雕细琢的语言。


<a id="chapter-5"></a>

## 第五章　文明进步：用长期趋势检验世界

### 5.1　进步的定义，以及新闻看不见的变化

> *来源：史蒂芬·平克：人性与人类进步。*

So what is progress? By progress I mean **improvements in human flourishing**, where human flourishing consists of qualities like life, health, sustenance, prosperity, peace, freedom, safety, knowledge, and happiness. These can be measured. If you plot them over time, and the good things go up and the bad things go down, that's what I mean by progress.

> 什么是进步？我指的是**人类福祉的改善**，包括生命、健康、食物供给、繁荣、和平、自由、安全、知识与幸福。这些都可以衡量。把它们随时间的变化画出来，好的指标上升，坏的指标下降，这就是我说的进步。

Now, has there been progress? Well, here's how not to tell whether there has been progress, is to follow the news. The news is a highly misleading way of perceiving trends in the world for a couple of reasons. One is that news is what happens, not what doesn't happen. And there are many, many more ways for things to go wrong than to go right. If something goes wrong, the news is a highly non-random sample, and it will present it to you. But if a part of the world has been at peace, if a city has not been attacked by terrorists, if there is not a famine or an epidemic in some part of the world, that's not news.

> 进步发生了吗？先说一个不适合判断的方法：追新闻。新闻很容易误导我们对世界趋势的认识，原因有几个。第一，新闻报道发生了什么，不报道没有发生什么；事情出错的方式又远多于顺利发展的方式。某件事出错，新闻这个高度非随机的样本就会呈现它。但某个地区一直和平，某座城市没有遭到恐怖袭击，某处没有饥荒或流行病，这些都不是新闻。

Also, news is what happens suddenly, not what happens gradually. Some things change the world a few percentage points a year, which then compound, but there's never a Thursday in October in which it happens, and so people who read the news every day or every hour or every minute can be completely ignorant of it. Also, news is what changes, not what persists.

> 此外，新闻报道突然发生的事情，不报道缓慢发生的事情。有些变化每年只让世界改善几个百分点，随后产生复利效应，却从来不会集中在十月某个星期四发生。因此，每天、每小时甚至每分钟看新闻的人，可能对此一无所知。新闻还关注变化，而不关注持续不变的趋势。

So let me give you a hypothetical example. Imagine there is some dimension of human well-being. Let's say this is life expectancy. So most years it goes up, but some years, nothing increases monotonically, let alone linearly. There are always wiggles and setbacks, even in this hypothetical example. Now, you're a journalist. Let's say life expectancy has gone up ten years in a row. Well, after year seven, that's not news, it's the same as last year. Year eight, that's not news, that's the same as last year. Year nine, that's not news. All of a sudden, it goes down. Well, that's news. For the first time, life expectancy has gone down. Well, then it resumes its upward climb, and again, boring, boring, boring, until the next time it reverses, and so that becomes news, and so on. And so the only thing that people ever read about are the setbacks, the reversals, the continuing improvement is completely invisible to someone who reads the news.

> 举个假想例子：某个人类福祉指标，比如预期寿命，大多数年份都在上升，但并非年年如此。没有什么永远单调增加，更别说直线上升，曲线总有波动和倒退。假设你是记者，预期寿命连续十年上升；到了第七年，这不再是新闻，因为和去年一样。第八年、第九年也不是新闻。突然某一年下降了，这就成了新闻：“预期寿命首次下降。”随后它恢复上升，又是一年又一年的无聊，直到下一次逆转，再次成为新闻。于是，读者看到的只有挫折与倒退，**持续改善对新闻读者而言完全不可见。**

And also, as a psychologist, I realized that the practice of journalism interacts with two cognitive biases to give us a distorted picture of trends. One of them is what is called the availability bias. This was discovered by Daniel Kahneman and Amos Tversky. Daniel Kahneman won a Nobel Prize for this and other discoveries and had a bestseller Thinking, Fast and Slow in which he explained it. The availability bias is that our sense of probability, of trends, of prevalence is determined by available anecdotes from memory, images, narratives, particular events that come to mind. **If it’s easy to remember something, we think that it is common.**

> 作为心理学家，我还意识到，新闻业的做法与两种认知偏差相互作用，使我们对趋势产生扭曲的印象。第一种叫“可得性偏差”，由丹尼尔·卡尼曼和阿莫斯·特沃斯基发现。卡尼曼凭这项和其他发现获得诺贝尔奖，并在畅销书《思考，快与慢》中作了解释。可得性偏差是指，我们对概率、趋势和普遍程度的感受，取决于记忆中容易提取的轶事、图像、叙述和具体事件。**某件事越容易想起来，我们就越觉得它常见。**

And the negativity bias, we are far more impressed by negative emotions, negative events than by positive ones. Probably for evolutionary reasons, you can be much more damaged by something that goes wrong than you can be helped by something that goes right. And so it makes sense to be more vigilant for the downside. Because of the interaction between the practice of journalism and these cognitive biases, there's a natural sense that everything is getting worse and always has been getting worse. A better way to tell whether there has been progress, and this I hope should be obvious, is to plot data on human well-being over time. And by doing that, there are constant pleasant surprises. Again, this is not optimism. This is not seeing the glass as half full. This is not looking on the bright side. *This is just looking at data instead of journalism.*

> 第二种是“负面偏差”：负面情绪和事件给我们的印象，远比正面事物强烈。这可能有进化原因：坏事造成的伤害，可能大于好事带来的收益，因此对坏的一面更加警惕有其道理。新闻实践与这些认知偏差结合，就会让我们自然觉得，一切都在恶化，而且一直在恶化。判断进步是否发生，更好的办法——我希望这已很明显——是把人类福祉的数据按时间画出来。这样做，会不断得到令人愉快的惊喜。再次强调，这不是乐观主义，不是把杯子看成半满，不是只看光明面，**只是看数据，而不是看新闻。**


### 5.2　生命、健康、食物与繁荣

> *来源：史蒂芬·平克：人性与人类进步。*

Life expectancy rose from 1950 to 2023, though the trend was not perfectly steady. COVID caused a setback, but the world recovered, and the figures for 2024 and 2025 were higher still. The improvement occurred not only in developed countries but in every region, including Africa. A large gap remains between Africa and the developed world, but life expectancy has continued to rise there too.

> 从 1950 年到 2023 年，预期寿命有所上升，但趋势并非完全平稳。新冠疫情造成了一次挫折，随后世界恢复，2024 年和 2025 年的数据还更高。改善不仅发生在发达国家，也发生在每个地区，包括非洲。非洲与发达世界仍有很大差距，但非洲的预期寿命也一直在上升。

Health also involves conditions that do not directly kill us. Data on years lost to death and disability over the last thirty-five years show a setback from COVID, followed by recovery.

> 健康还涉及那些不直接致命的问题。过去三十五年因死亡和残疾损失的寿命年数据表明，新冠疫情造成了一次倒退，随后又有所恢复。

When I was growing up and attending college, predictions of widespread famine were common. In *The Population Bomb*, biologist Paul Ehrlich predicted with great confidence that hundreds of millions would die in worldwide famines in the 1970s. The argument, associated with Thomas Malthus, was that population grows exponentially while food production grows linearly. In fact, while population increased over the last two hundred years, deaths from famine decreased. In that respect, Malthus and Ehrlich were wrong.

> 我成长和上大学时，大规模饥荒的预言十分普遍。生物学家保罗·埃利希在《人口爆炸》中十分笃定地预测，二十世纪七十年代的全球饥荒会造成数亿人死亡。这沿用了托马斯·马尔萨斯的论证：人口呈指数增长，而粮食产量只呈线性增长。事实上，过去两百年人口增加的同时，饥荒死亡人数却减少了。在这一点上，马尔萨斯和埃利希错了。

Undernourishment means consuming fewer calories than necessary, rather than dying from famine. It has declined in most regions of the world. There has, however, been a reversal in Africa, where nutrition has moved in the wrong direction over the last ten to fifteen years.

> 营养不足指热量摄入低于所需，并不等于死于饥荒。世界多数地区的营养不足都减少了。不过，非洲出现了倒退：过去十到十五年，营养状况朝错误方向发展。

Economic historians have traced gross world product over two millennia. For the first fifteen hundred years, growth was barely visible on that scale. After the Industrial Revolution, global prosperity increased about two hundredfold. Growth continued in recent years, with a setback from COVID followed by recovery.

> 经济史学家追踪了两千年来的世界生产总值。在这一尺度上，最初一千五百年的增长几乎难以察觉。工业革命之后，全球繁荣水平增长了约两百倍。近些年增长仍在继续；新冠疫情造成一次挫折，随后又有所恢复。

A cynic might say that all the gains went to the richest 1%, with billionaires pulling up the average. But prosperity has spread more widely. Measured using a constant definition, constant dollars, and purchasing power, extreme poverty fell from about 90% of humanity two hundred years ago to less than 9%. The historical series through 2015, together with more recent data, shows that extreme poverty continued to decline.

> 犬儒者或许会说，增长全归最富的 1%，平均值只是被亿万富翁拉高了。但繁荣也扩展到了其他人群。用固定定义、不变美元与购买力衡量，极端贫困人口比例从两百年前的约 90% 降到了不足 9%。截至 2015 年的历史序列及更近的数据表明，极端贫困仍在继续下降。


### 5.3　和平、权利、安全、知识与幸福

> *来源：史蒂芬·平克：人性与人类进步。*

The twentieth century saw two horrific peaks in war deaths, corresponding to the world wars. Since World War II, the global death rate from wars of all kinds has fluctuated greatly but declined overall. Recent wars in Ukraine, Gaza, Sudan, and Ethiopia have partly reversed that decline, taking the rate back toward late-1980s levels. That is a serious setback, though still below the levels of earlier periods such as the 1950s and 1970s.

> 二十世纪出现过两次可怕的战争死亡高峰，对应两次世界大战。二战以后，全球各类战争的死亡率虽大幅波动，总体仍有所下降。近期乌克兰、加沙、苏丹和埃塞俄比亚的战争部分逆转了下降趋势，使死亡率回到接近二十世纪八十年代末的水平。这是严重的倒退，但仍低于五十年代、七十年代等更早时期的水平。

Organizations that track democracy use continuous scales rather than simply dividing countries into democracies and autocracies. Their measures show a substantial rise from 1950 to 2023, with some decline in recent years in countries including Russia, Turkey, Hungary, and the United States. The overall situation remains better than when half of Europe was behind the Iron Curtain and dictatorships ruled Spain, Portugal, Greece, much of Latin America, and South Korea. Measures of women’s empowerment likewise rose before slipping somewhat in recent years. Indices of LGBTQ rights show improvement on several criteria.

> 追踪民主程度的机构使用连续尺度，而不只是把国家分成民主与专制两类。这些指标显示，1950 至 2023 年有显著提升，近些年俄罗斯、土耳其、匈牙利、美国等国则出现下滑。总体情况仍好于半个欧洲处于铁幕后、西班牙、葡萄牙、希腊、拉丁美洲许多国家及韩国由独裁政权统治的时代。女性赋权指标同样先有上升，近些年略有下降；LGBTQ 权利指数则在多个标准上有所改善。

Violent crime has fallen sharply since the Middle Ages in Italy, the Netherlands, Germany, England, and Scandinavia. Over the last two decades, the global homicide rate has shown a gradual decline.

> 从中世纪以来，意大利、荷兰、德国、英格兰和斯堪的纳维亚的暴力犯罪大幅减少。近二十年来，全球凶杀率呈现缓慢下降的趋势。

> *来源：这个世界在变好吗？。*

Indeed, we've become safer. In the last century, we've become 96 percent less likely to be killed in a car crash, 88 percent less likely to be mowed down on the sidewalk, 99 percent less likely to die in a plane crash, 95 percent less likely to be killed on the job, and 89 percent less likely to be killed by an act of God, such as a drought, flood, wildfire, storm, volcano, landslide, earthquake, or meteor strike—presumably **NOT** because God has become less angry with us, **BUT** because of improvements in the resilience of our infrastructure. And what about the quintessential act of God, the projectile hurled by Zeus himself? Yes, we are 97 percent less likely to be killed by a bolt of lightning.

> 事实上，我们的生活更安全了。在过去一个世纪里，死于车祸的概率下降了 96%，在人行道上被撞死的概率下降了 88%，死于飞机失事的概率下降了 99%，因工死亡的概率下降了 95%，死于旱灾、洪灾、野火、风暴、火山、滑坡、地震或陨石撞击等天灾的概率下降了 89%。这想必**不是**因为上帝对我们的怒气减轻了，**而是**因为基础设施抵御灾害的能力提高了。那么，最典型的“神的行为”——宙斯亲手投出的雷电——又如何呢？是的，遭雷击死亡的概率也下降了 97%。

In the nineteenth century, Westerners worked more than sixty hours per week. Today, they work fewer than forty. Thanks to the universal penetration of running water and electricity in the developed world, and the widespread adoption of washing machines, vacuum cleaners, refrigerators, dishwashers, stoves, and microwaves, the amount of our lives that we forfeit to housework has fallen from sixty hours a week to fewer than fifteen hours a week.

> 十九世纪，西方人每周工作超过六十小时，如今则不到四十小时。由于发达世界普及了自来水和电力，洗衣机、吸尘器、冰箱、洗碗机、炉灶和微波炉也得到广泛应用，我们花在家务上的时间，从每周六十小时降到了不到十五小时。

> *来源：史蒂芬·平克：人性与人类进步。*

Knowledge, we are naturally illiterate. Unlike spoken language, which, as I argued, can be seen as an instinct, and as Darwin noted, no child has an instinctive tendency to write. This has to be learned. But over the course of history, over the course of that period,[^progress-literacy] literacy has gone from about 15% of humanity to more than 90% of humanity. And a basic education, that is at least some formal schooling, again, that has gone from about 10% of humanity to more than 80%.

> 再看知识。人类天生不识字。与可以视为本能的口语不同，正如达尔文所说，没有孩子天生倾向于写字，读写必须学习。但在历史发展中，全球识字人口比例由约 15% 上升到超过 90%。[^progress-literacy] 基础教育，也就是至少接受过一些正规学校教育的人口比例，也从约 10% 上升到 80% 以上。

Finally, happiness: what would these improvements mean if they did not make us happier? We lack happiness surveys from centuries ago. But richer countries generally tend to be happier. Among countries with at least two measurements since the 1980s, a majority—though **NOT ALL**—became happier over the following forty years.

> 最后是幸福：如果这些改善没有让我们更幸福，又有什么意义？我们没有几个世纪以前的幸福感调查。不过，一般而言，较富裕的国家也往往更幸福。自二十世纪八十年代以来至少有过两次测量的国家中，多数——但**并非全部**——在此后四十年变得更幸福了。

More recent data, separating people aged fifteen to twenty-four from those twenty-five and older, suggest that the world became somewhat happier overall. There is considerable variation across countries and demographic groups, including many groups whose happiness declined. Another indicator concerns suicide: the global suicide rate has fallen by about 40% since 1980.[^progress-suicide]

> 更近的数据把十五至二十四岁与二十五岁以上的人分开考察，结果表明，全球总体幸福感略有提高。不过，国家与人口群体之间差异很大，其中也有许多群体的幸福感下降。另一项指标涉及自杀：自 1980 年以来，全球自杀率下降了约 40%。[^progress-suicide]

So just to summarize 14 global trends that I've briefly mentioned, in the last 20 years or so, 10 of them, the historical progress has continued, life, health, prosperity, poverty, gay rights, homicide, literacy, education and happiness, and suicide. Some of the trends have stalled or even are somewhat backtracking, women's rights since 2010, nutrition since 2005 to 2010, democracy has been in a quantitative decline since 2000, peace since 1990, but the number of trends where the progress has been erased, that is we're worse off now than we were in the 20th century, zero.

> 概括刚才简要介绍的十四项全球趋势，近二十年左右，其中十项延续了历史进步：寿命、健康、繁荣、贫困改善、同性恋者权利、凶杀减少、识字率、教育、幸福和自杀减少。一些趋势停滞，甚至有所倒退：女性权利从 2010 年起，营养从 2005 至 2010 年间起，民主程度从 2000 年起出现量化下降，和平从 1990 年起倒退。但进步完全被抹去、以至于现在比二十世纪更差的趋势，有多少？零。


### 5.4　进步与平等：我们究竟在衡量什么

> *来源：史蒂芬·平克：人性与人类进步。*

My question is regarding the notions of progress. The ones you mentioned, like sustenance and poverty, I think we can all agree on: if the needle moves in the right direction, then that is progress. But there are also different categories and topics that might be a bit more complex, like equality: how many women are working in STEM, how many men are working in care jobs. Can we all come to a common agreement, also on these complex topics, about where the needle should move?

> 我的问题有关进步概念。食物供给、贫困这些指标，我想大家都能同意，只要朝正确方向变化，就是进步。但还有其他更复杂的类别，比如平等，有多少女性从事 STEM 工作，有多少男性从事照护工作。对于这些复杂问题，我们能否形成共识，认定指标应该朝什么方向移动？

Yes, it is certainly true that the question of whether progress has happened has to be relative to your criterion for progress, and there can be disagreements. I listed criteria for progress that I felt were pretty uncontroversial, like it’s better to be alive than dead, better not to starve than to starve, better to be literate than illiterate. But there can be disagreements. For example, is economic equality a criterion of progress? If the world has gotten richer but more unequal—let’s say poor people are less poor, but rich people are way, way richer—is that inherently bad? There you do have a value judgment. In Enlightenment Now, I suggested that inequality per se is not a good criterion for progress. The appropriate criterion is **how well do people at the bottom do**, not what is the gap between people at the top and the bottom.

> 确实，判断进步是否发生，必须相对于你采用的进步标准，而标准可能存在分歧。我列举的是我认为争议很少的标准，例如活着比死去好，不挨饿比挨饿好，识字比文盲好。但也可以有争论。比如经济平等是不是进步标准？如果世界更富裕却更不平等，穷人没那么穷了，富人却富裕了许多许多，这本身是不是坏事？这里就涉及价值判断。我在《当下的启蒙》中提出，不平等本身并不是好的进步标准，适当的标准是**底层人的生活有多好，而不是顶层与底层的差距有多大。**

That should be obvious in the case of, say, longevity. If some people live to a hundred and other people only make it to seventy, is that worse than if everyone dies at fifty, or forty, or thirty? I think everyone would agree: no. It’s better if the people with the shortest lives live to seventy. Who cares if some people are living to a hundred? Living to seventy is better than living to fifty. I would argue the same thing is true of wealth: economic inequality is not the issue; poverty is the issue. Other people would disagree, but that’s just one example of how the question ‘Has there been progress?’ does depend on what you count as progress. My argument is that there are so many things that are not controversial that we can meaningfully ask the question, simply because there is so much agreement: it’s better to be alive than dead, healthy than sick, well-fed than starving, literate than illiterate. At the margins there can be disagreements.

> 拿寿命来说，这应该很明显：有些人活到一百岁，另一些只活到七十岁，难道比所有人五十岁、四十岁或三十岁就死去更糟吗？我想大家会说，不是；最短寿的人也能活到七十岁更好，有些人活到一百岁又何妨？活到七十岁总比五十岁好。我认为财富也是一样，问题不在经济不平等，而在贫困。当然，别人可能不同意。这只是说明，“有没有进步”确实取决于你把什么算作进步。我的论点是，不存在争议的方面已经很多，足以让这个问题有意义，因为我们对活着胜过死去、健康胜过疾病、吃饱胜过饥饿、识字胜过文盲已有广泛共识。边缘问题可以有分歧。


### 5.5　改善来自努力，也可能被逆转

> *来源：这个世界在变好吗？。*

Isn't it good to be pessimistic, to safeguard against complacency, to rake the muck, to speak truth to power? Well, **NOT** exactly. *It's good to be accurate.* Of course, we should be aware of suffering and danger wherever they occur, but we should also be aware of how they can be reduced, because there are dangers to indiscriminate pessimism.

> 保持悲观不是好事吗？它能防止自满、揭露黑幕、向权力说真话。答案是，**不完全如此**。*真正的好事是准确地认识现实。*当然，无论痛苦和危险发生在哪里，我们都应该关注，但也应该知道如何减少它们，因为不加区分的悲观本身就有危险。

One of them is fatalism. If all our efforts at improving the world have been in vain, why throw good money after bad? “The poor will always be with you.” And since the world will end soon—if climate change doesn't kill us all, then runaway artificial intelligence will—a natural response is to enjoy life while we can: eat, drink, and be merry, for tomorrow we die.

> 其中一个危险是宿命论。如果我们改善世界的一切努力都徒劳无功，为什么还要继续把钱打水漂？“穷人总会与你们同在。”而且，既然世界很快就要结束——即使气候变化不会杀死所有人，失控的人工智能也会——那么一种自然反应就是趁还能享受时及时行乐：吃喝玩乐吧，因为明天就要死了。

The other danger of thoughtless pessimism is radicalism. If our institutions are all failing and beyond hope for reform, a natural response is to seek to smash the machine, drain the swamp, burn the empire to the ground, in the hope that whatever rises out of the ashes is bound to be better than what we have now.

> 未经思考的悲观主义还有另一个危险：激进主义。如果所有制度都在失败，而且改革已经无望，那么自然的反应就是砸烂机器、抽干沼泽、把帝国烧成灰烬，寄希望于从灰烬中诞生的任何东西，都一定比现状更好。

Well, if there is such a thing as progress, what causes it? Progress is **NOT** some mystical force or dialectic lifting us ever higher. It's **NOT** a mysterious arc of history bending toward justice. It's the result of human efforts governed by an idea—an idea that we associate with the eighteenth-century Enlightenment: **if we apply reason and science to enhance human well-being, we can gradually succeed.**

> 那么，如果确实存在进步，是什么带来了它？进步**不是**某种将我们不断托高的神秘力量或辩证法，**也不是**一条神秘地向正义弯曲的历史弧线。它是人类在一种观念指导下努力的结果。这种观念与十八世纪启蒙运动相联系：**如果运用理性和科学来增进人类福祉，我们就能逐步取得成功。**

Is progress inevitable? Of course **NOT**. Progress does **NOT** mean that everything becomes better for everyone, everywhere, all the time. That would be a miracle, and progress is **NOT** a miracle, **BUT** problem-solving. Problems are inevitable, and solutions create new problems which have to be solved in their turn. The unsolved problems facing the world today are gargantuan, including the risks of climate change and nuclear war, but we **MUST** see them as problems to be solved, **NOT** apocalypses in waiting, and aggressively pursue solutions like deep decarbonization for climate change and global zero for nuclear war.

> 进步不可避免吗？当然**不是**。进步**不意味着**每个人、每个地方、每时每刻，一切都在变好。那将是奇迹，而进步**不是**奇迹，**而是**解决问题。问题不可避免，解决方案又会带来需要进一步解决的新问题。世界今天面对的未解难题极其庞大，包括气候变化和核战争的风险。但我们**必须**将它们视为需要解决的问题，**而不是**正在等待降临的末日，并积极寻求解决办法，比如以深度脱碳应对气候变化，以全球消除核武器应对核战争风险。

> *来源：为什么聪明的人会相信愚蠢的事情：英语原声与逐段中译。*

**Those are two different arguments.** One of them is that Enlightenment values increase. And the other is that Enlightenment values cause the world to be better as measured by actual outcomes. The – there's no law that says they have to keep increasing. We know that there are – the values that is, the whole Enlightenment ideals, in some ways do go against features of human nature like tribalism, authoritarianism, puritanism, magical thinking, primitive intuitions of essentialism.

> 这是**两个不同的论点**：一个是启蒙价值观扩展了；另一个是这些价值观让实际可测量的世界状况变好了。没有任何定律保证它们必然继续扩展。启蒙理想在某些方面确实违背人性中的部落主义、威权倾向、清教徒式道德观、巫术思维和本质主义直觉。

And authoritarianism is, you think, sort of part of human nature. I mean that's our general instinct. Yeah, sort of. Deference to what people think of as a legitimate authority is probably one of the ways in which we moralize the world. Jonathan Haidt in his Moral Foundations includes deference to authority as kind of one of the keys available on the keyboard. Not everywhere always, but I think it's something that people tend to fall into. How do you solve our problems? Well, let's entrust a strong leader. So we're vulnerable to that.

> 你认为威权主义某种程度上也是人性的一部分，是我们的普遍本能？大致如此。服从被视为正当的权威，可能是人们给世界赋予道德意义的一种方式。乔纳森·海特的道德基础理论，把服从权威看成道德键盘上的一个琴键。不是时时处处如此，但人们容易走向那里：问题怎么解决？托付给一个强有力的领袖。我们容易受这种想法吸引。

The whole concept of democracy is no, it's not that a strong leader just has the wisdom and strength to solve problems. It's that we need to agree upon someone to temporarily preside over a council, a president, **NOT** a king. And that person wields power only for the betterment of the people that he or she is governing. The whole concept of democracy is not particularly intuitive. I think it's intuitive to challenge a leader who's arrogated too much power to take him out. But consent of the governed, division of powers, and freedom of speech are completely unintuitive.

> 民主的理念恰恰**不是**说，一个强人拥有解决问题的全部智慧与力量；**而是**我们共同同意，让某人暂时主持公共事务。他是总统，不是国王，行使权力只是为了被治理者的福祉。民主整体并不特别符合直觉。挑战并推翻攫取过多权力的领袖，可能符合直觉；但被统治者同意、权力分立、言论自由，则完全不是自然而然就有的。

So enlightenment values, I think, are always pushing uphill and have a natural tendency to slide back. If I'm right, and I don't have the data to demonstrate this empirically, but the idea would be as the world retreats from enlightenment values, so too measures of global well-being will go backwards.

> 所以我认为，启蒙价值观一直是在逆坡而上，也天然容易滑落。如果我说得对——我还没有数据作实证证明——那么随着世界背离启蒙价值观，全球福祉指标也会倒退。


### 5.6　制度如何约束人性中的阴暗面

> *来源：史蒂芬·平克：人性与人类进步。*

The **dark triad** comprises narcissism, Machiavellianism, and psychopathy. Only a minority of people have these traits, but some seek political power. One essential function of democracy is to prevent such people from accumulating too much power. The American founders called them tyrants; the problem was already familiar in ancient Greece and Rome. Constitutions deliberately build safeguards against this danger. An important debate in the United States is how well those safeguards are holding.

> **黑暗三联征**包括自恋、马基雅维利主义和精神病态。只有少数人具有这些特征，但其中一些人追求政治权力。民主的一项重要功能，是防止这类人掌握过多权力。美国建国者称他们为暴君；古希腊和古罗马就已熟悉这个问题。宪法有意识地设置制度防线，来应对这种危险。美国的一项重要争论是，这些防线究竟运作得怎样。

Now, you asked about people acting collectively. Well, they're only a minority that will have the dark triad. There's some reason to think that at least some of our cognitive flaws can be mitigated when people have to deliberate in groups. That's kind of the rationale behind the wisdom of crowds, behind democracy, behind organizations like scientific societies and scholarly societies. The TNG. And companies. That every individual has cognitive bias and limitations. All of us think we're right. Not all of us can be right. But we don't recognize our own biases, but we're much better at recognizing the other guy's biases. And so if you have people in a forum with, crucially, with **freedom of speech**, so you don't get punished for criticizing someone else's idea, that holds out the hope that a whole community with norms of open criticism, open debate, can be smarter, more rational than any of the individuals in it.

> 至于集体行动，毕竟具有黑暗三联征的只是少数人。有理由认为，当人们必须在群体中审议时，至少部分认知缺陷会减轻。这是群体智慧、民主、科学与学术团体、TNG 和公司这类组织背后的道理。每个人都有认知偏差与局限。我们都觉得自己正确，但不可能人人都正确。我们不容易察觉自己的偏差，却更善于发现别人的偏差。因此，把人放在一个论坛里，**关键是保证言论自由，使人不会因批评别人的想法而受罚**，就有希望让一个具有公开批评、公开辩论规范的共同体，比其中任何个人都更聪明、更理性。


### 5.7　把环境问题拆开，寻找可行的解决办法

> *来源：史蒂芬·平克：人性与人类进步。*

Climate change needs to be treated as a distinct problem. Seeing every difficulty as part of one connected collapse is not how we make progress. *Progress comes from dividing up problems.* Many measures of environmental quality have improved. In virtually every developed country, air pollutants such as nitrogen oxides, carbon monoxide, sulfur dioxide, and particulate matter have declined. Water quality has improved, protected areas have improved, and deforestation has slowed.

> 气候变化需要作为一个具体问题来处理。把所有困难视为一场相互关联的全面崩溃，并不是实现进步的办法。*进步来自把问题拆开处理。*许多环境质量指标已有改善。几乎所有发达国家的氮氧化物、一氧化碳、二氧化硫和颗粒物等空气污染物都有所减少；水质有所改善，保护区有所改善，森林砍伐速度减缓。

Greenhouse gases have not gone in the right direction, to put it mildly. But we're coming to see that the worst-case scenarios, the scenarios that were on the verge of extinction, turn out to be a very extreme case engineered for maximum pessimism, namely that the world would go on an orgy of burning coal, and population would continue to increase exponentially. And because population is leveling off, possibly decreasing, and because the rate of adoption of coal has not accelerated, I think last year more new solar was added than coal, the worst-case scenarios, the 8.5 scenario is now considered to be extremely unlikely. That doesn't mean that the news about climate is good. It is **DEFINITELY BAD**.

> 温室气体却没有朝正确方向发展，这已经是委婉的说法。但我们开始意识到，那些让人觉得接近灭绝的最坏情景，其实是按极度悲观假设构造的极端情景：世界疯狂燃煤，人口持续指数增长。 如今人口增长正在趋平，甚至可能减少，煤炭使用增长也没有加速；我想去年新增太阳能比新增煤电更多。因此，最坏的 8.5 情景现在被认为极不可能。这不意味着气候消息是好消息，**它显然是坏消息**。

But we also have reasons to believe that solutions exist. That is, if there is an acceleration of technologies that deliver abundant clean energy. This may be controversial in Germany, but I think it’ll become less and less controversial: nuclear power will have to be part of that. We’re seeing that the world’s appetite for energy is pretty much limitless. Ten years ago, the concept of the energy consumption of AI wasn’t even a concept, or of crypto. Now we know that, together with all the other uses for energy, there are these two new ones. So we have an idea of what the solution will have to look like. It’s not going to be voluntary conservation. No one’s going to do that.

> 但我们也有理由相信存在解决办法：加速发展可以提供充足清洁能源的技术。 这在德国可能有争议，但我认为争议会越来越小：核能必须是其中一部分。我们正在看到，世界对能源的需求几乎没有止境。十年前，人工智能的能耗甚至还不是一个议题，加密货币也是如此；现在，在所有其他能源用途之外，又增加了这两项。我们知道解决办法大概应该是什么样：它不会依靠自愿节约能源，没人会这么做。

Especially not China and India. The solution has to be making clean energy **CHEAPER and MORE ABUNDANT than dirty energy**, so people adopt clean energy in their own interests. We’re not living in that world yet, but that is the aspiration; that has to be the goal. I don’t want to say it’s going to happen. Maybe it won’t happen, but it’s possible.

> 尤其中国和印度不会。解决办法必须是让清洁能源比污染能源**更便宜、更充足**，让人们出于自身利益选择清洁能源。我们还没有生活在这样的世界，但它必须是愿景、必须是目标。我不想断言这一定会发生；也许不会，但它是可能的。


<a id="chapter-6"></a>

## 第六章　人工智能与未来：能力、目标和风险

### 6.1　意识、智能与模拟人脑的区别

> *来源：Lex Fridman 访谈史蒂芬·平克：恐惧由想象力驱动，而非数据（2018 年 10 月）。*

Compared to artificial neural networks, which we use for machine learning today, is there something fundamentally more complex, mysterious, even magical about the biological neural networks versus the ones we’ve been starting to use over the past 60 years and have come to success in the past 10?

> 与今天机器学习使用的人工神经网络相比，生物神经网络是否在根本上更加复杂、神秘，甚至具有某种魔力？毕竟，人工神经网络我们已经研究了六十年，而过去十年才取得显著成功。

There is something a little bit mysterious about the human neural networks, which is that each one of us who is a neural network knows that we ourselves are conscious. Conscious not in the sense of registering our surroundings or even registering our internal state, but in having subjective first-person present tense experience. That is, when I see red, it's not just different from green, but there's a redness to it that I feel. Whether an artificial system would experience that or not, I don't know, and I don't think I can know. That's why it's mysterious. If we had a perfectly lifelike robot that was behaviorally indistinguishable from a human, would we attribute consciousness to it, or ought we to attribute consciousness to it? And that's something that it's very hard to know.

> 人类神经网络确实有一点神秘之处：我们每个人都是一个神经网络，却知道自己具有意识。这里的意识不只是记录周围环境，甚至也不只是记录内部状态，而是具有主观的、第一人称的、当下的体验。比如，我看到红色时，不只是把它与绿色区分开，而是确实感受到一种“红”。人工系统是否也会有这种体验，我不知道，也不认为自己能够知道。这就是神秘所在。如果有一个与真人行为完全无法区分、栩栩如生的机器人，我们会赋予它意识吗？或者，我们应当认为它有意识吗？这很难判断。

But putting that largely philosophical question aside, is there some difference between the human neural network and the ones that we're building in artificial intelligence? Will that mean that our trajectory is not going to reach the point where we've got a lifelike robot indistinguishable from a human because their so-called neural networks are organized differently from ours? I think there's overlap, but I think there are some big differences. Current neural networks, current so-called deep learning systems, are in reality not all that deep. That is, they are very good at extracting high-order statistical regularities. But most of the systems don't have a semantic level, a level of actual understanding of who did what to whom, why, where, how things work, what causes what else.

> 但先把这个主要属于哲学的问题放在一边。人类神经网络与我们在人工智能中构建的网络，是否存在某种差异，使我们沿当前路线无法造出与人无法区分的逼真机器人？也就是说，它们所谓神经网络的组织方式，与我们的组织方式不同。我认为两者有重叠，也有重大差异。目前的神经网络、所谓深度学习系统，其实并没有那么“深”。它们很善于提取高阶统计规律，但大多数系统缺乏语义层面，也就是没有真正理解谁对谁做了什么、为什么、在哪里，以及事物如何运作、什么导致什么。

Do you think that kind of thing can emerge as it does? So artificial neural networks are much smaller, the number of connections and so on, than the current human biological networks. But do you think sort of to go to consciousness or to go to this higher level semantic reasoning about things, do you think that can emerge with just a larger network, a more richly, weirdly interconnected network?

> 你认为这种能力能否涌现？人工神经网络的规模，包括连接数量，比人脑的生物网络小得多。那么，通往意识或更高层次语义推理的能力，是否可能仅通过更大的、连接更丰富也更奇特的网络涌现出来？

Separate out, again, consciousness, because consciousness isn't even a matter of complexity. It's a really weird one. Yeah, you could sensibly ask the question of whether shrimp are conscious, for example. They're not terribly complex, but maybe they feel pain. So let's just put that part of it aside. But I think **sheer size of a neural network is NOT enough to give it structure and knowledge**. But if it’s suitably engineered, then why not? That is, we're neural networks. Natural selection did a kind of equivalent of engineering of our brains. So I don't think there's anything mysterious in the sense that no system made out of silicon could ever do what a human brain can do. I think it's possible in principle. Whether it'll ever happen depends not only on how clever we are in engineering these systems, but whether we even want to, whether that's even a sensible goal.

> 还是要把意识单独拿出来，因为意识甚至不一定是复杂性问题。它真的很奇怪。比如，问虾有没有意识，是一个合理的问题：虾并不特别复杂，但也许能感到疼痛。因此先把意识放到一边。我认为，**单纯扩大神经网络的规模，并不足以赋予它结构和知识**。但如果工程设计合适，为什么不可能呢？我们自己就是神经网络，自然选择在某种意义上相当于对大脑进行了工程设计。因此，我不认为存在什么神秘障碍，使硅构成的系统永远无法做人脑能做的事。我认为原则上是可能的。至于将来是否会发生，不只取决于我们设计系统的聪明程度，还取决于我们是否想这样做，这个目标是否合理。

That is, you can ask the question: is there any locomotion system that is as good as a human? Well, we kind of want to do better than a human, ultimately, in terms of legged locomotion. There's no reason that humans should be our benchmark. There are tools that might be better in some ways. It may be that we can't duplicate a natural system because at some point it's so much cheaper to use a natural system that we're not going to invest more brain power and resources. So, for example, we don't really have an exact substitute for wood. We still build houses out of wood. We still build furniture out of wood. We like the look. We like the feel. Wood has certain properties that synthetics don't. It's not that there's anything magical or mysterious about wood. It's just that the extra steps of duplicating everything about wood is something we just haven't bothered because we have wood.

> 你可以问：有没有像人一样好的运动系统？但最终，我们希望有腿的运动系统比人更好，没理由一定以人为基准。工具在某些方面可能做得更好。也可能到某个阶段，直接使用自然系统便宜得多，我们就不会继续投入脑力和资源去完全复制它。例如，我们并没有木材的精确替代品，仍用木材造房子、做家具，喜欢它的外观和触感。木材有一些合成材料没有的特性。这不是因为木材有什么魔力或神秘之处，只是完全复制木材的额外步骤，我们懒得去做，因为已经有木材了。

Likewise, cotton. I'm wearing cotton clothing now. It feels much better than polyester. It's not that cotton has something magic in it. And it's not that we couldn't ever synthesize something exactly like cotton. But at some point, it's just not worth it. We've got cotton. And likewise, in the case of human intelligence, the goal of making an artificial system that is exactly like the human brain is a goal that probably no one is going to pursue to the bitter end, I suspect. Because if you want tools that do things better than humans, you're not going to care whether it does something like humans. So for example, diagnosing cancer or predicting the weather, why set humans as your benchmark?

> 棉花也是如此。我现在穿的就是棉质衣服，感觉比聚酯纤维舒服得多。棉花没有什么魔力，也不是说我们永远无法合成与棉花完全一样的东西；只是到某个程度，就不值得再投入了，因为我们已经有棉花。同样，我怀疑不会有人把制造一个与人脑完全相同的人工系统作为目标，一直追求到底。因为如果你想要的是比人做得更好的工具，就不会在乎它是否用人的方式工作。例如诊断癌症或预测天气，为什么要把人设为基准？

But in general, I suspect you also believe that even if the human should not be a benchmark and we don't want to imitate humans in their system, there's a lot to be learned about how to create an artificial intelligence system by studying the human.

> 不过总体而言，我想你也相信，即使不以人为基准、不想让系统模仿人，研究人类仍然能让我们学到很多构建人工智能系统的方法。

Yeah, I think that's right. In the same way that to build flying machines, we want to understand the laws of aerodynamics. And including birds, but not mimic the birds. But they're the same laws.

> 是的，我想是这样。就像制造飞行器时，我们需要理解空气动力学规律，包括鸟类所遵循的规律，但不必模仿鸟。它们遵循的是同样的规律。


### 6.2　大语言模型改变了什么

> *来源：如何在 AI 时代写作：史蒂芬·平克谈清晰表达与语言之美。*

Interviewer: If clichés and abstractions can make it harder to think originally, what happens when AI makes them even easier to produce? Will large language models extend that trend, or create new ways of communicating?

> 主持人：如果套话和抽象表达会削弱原创思考，那么人工智能让它们更容易产生以后，会发生什么？大语言模型会延续这种趋势，还是创造新的沟通方式？

The output of LLMs is peculiar. In one sense, it's well-written in the sense that it tends not to be in academese, or jargon. The sentence structure tends to be pretty plain and sound. Even the progression of ideas tends to be orderly. There tends to be an introductory sentence and concluding sentence. So in that sense, it's good writing. It's bad in the sense that **it is so generic and prosaic.** Mm-hmm. you could almost recognize the output of a large language model. It's so banal. Now, perhaps it'd be trained, and perhaps if you prompted it, don't be prosaic. Don't be plain. It'd be interesting to see whether it would come up with any kind of style or freshness. But it's not the way it's designed. It's designed as a mashup, as a pastiche of, literally, billions of examples out there. And it’s an interesting question why its prose style should at least be sound. I think that's the best you could say about it. Whereas the prose style of most, academics, most lawyers, most bureaucrats is not sound.

> 平克：大语言模型的输出很奇特。某种意义上，它写得不错：通常没有学究腔或行话，句子结构往往朴实、稳妥，思想的展开也有条理，通常有引入句和总结句。从这个角度看，是好写作。但另一方面，它又很糟，因为**太泛化、太平淡、太陈腐**，你几乎能认出来这是大语言模型的作品。也许可以训练它，或者在提示里说“不要平淡，不要平实”，看看能不能产生风格或新鲜感，这会很有意思。但这并不是它原本的设计方式，它的设计是把数十亿个例子混合、拼接起来。一个有趣的问题是：它的行文为什么至少还算稳妥？我认为这已经是对它最好的评价了，而多数学者、律师或官僚的文风却并不稳妥。

Why is it better? One possibility is it's just been hammered into shape through the fine-tuning and the feedback. That is, instead of just regurgitating an amalgam—Host: The reinforcement learning. Pinker: The reinforcement learning, and the stuff they don’t really talk about much but that might be essential. That is, there are real human beings who force it into a five-paragraph essay. Right.

> 为什么它反而更好？一种可能是，它经过微调和反馈，被反复锤炼成现在的样子，而不是只吐出一团混合物。主持人：强化学习。平克：对，强化学习，还有那些他们不太常提、却可能很关键的工作：真实的人把它约束成一篇五段式文章。

The other possibility—and this is completely speculative—is this. We know in visual beauty, often a composite is more attractive than the elements that went into the composite. So that if you take a bunch of faces and morph them together, the non-existent human being that comes out of the morphing is more handsome or prettier than all of the men's faces and women's faces that went into it. Host: Really? Pinker: Take a high school yearbook and you mash together the several hundred faces and the result is pretty attractive. Is that right? Yeah. Wow.

> 平克：另一个想法完全是猜测。我们知道，在视觉美感方面，合成的整体往往比原来的组成部分更有吸引力。如果把许多张脸融合在一起，生成一个并不存在的人，这个人可能比参与合成的男脸、女脸都更英俊或漂亮。主持人：真的？平克：拿一本高中毕业纪念册，把几百张脸合成起来，结果往往很好看。主持人：是吗？哇。平克：是的。

Now, could that be true of prose style? If you eliminated all the god-awful convoluted constructions and came up with a generic sentence structure, it wouldn’t be beautiful, but it would be clear. **That’s a hypothesis.**

> 这会不会也适用于文章风格？如果去掉那些糟糕透顶、盘根错节的结构，得到一种普通的句子结构，也许称不上美，却可以很清楚。**这只是一个假说。**

Interviewer: Knowing what we now know about large language models, would you write the same books? How would you change them?

> 主持人：根据我们现在对大语言模型的了解，你还会写出同样的书吗？会作哪些改变？

Probably not. I think I’d have to give greater weight to the power of abstracting patterns from massive amounts of input, The approach I was trained in and then developed—computational cognitive science, Chomsky and linguistics, classic AI—was much more organized around rules, algorithms, logic, and logical programming. It was hard to imagine how, with enough just sheer input and training, an associative neural network could extract sensible ideas and prose out of this huge, unstructured mass of input.

> 平克：大概不会。我会更重视从海量输入中抽取模式的力量。我受训并进一步发展的研究路径——计算认知科学、乔姆斯基的语言学、经典人工智能——更多围绕规则、算法、逻辑和逻辑编程展开。过去很难想象，仅仅凭借足够多的输入与训练，一个联想式神经网络，就能从庞大、无结构的输入中提取出有意义的思想和文章。

Now, I do **NOT** think that shows that the human mind is a large language model, because it would be the equivalent of a child listening to language for, 30,000 years before they could put up their first sentence, clearly. Right. And also, kids don't just need to have massive amounts of text pour into them. They are also situated in a world where they can figure out what the people talking to them are trying to say. And they're interacting with the world, and that's a very different style of learning than just processing massive amounts of text. But still, I certainly would have had to reconcile the intelligence of large language models with human intelligence, with more attention to the power of pattern extraction from large input corpora than I did.

> 但我**不认为**这说明人类心智就是一个大语言模型。那相当于一个孩子听了三万年语言，才能说出第一个句子，显然不符合人类的情况。此外，孩子并不只是被灌入海量文字；他们置身于一个世界中，能够推断与他们说话的人想表达什么，也会与世界互动。这与单纯处理海量文本，是非常不同的学习方式。不过，我确实需要重新协调对大语言模型智能和人类智能的解释，比过去更重视从大型输入语料中抽取模式的力量。


### 6.3　创造力：旧观念的新组合

> *来源：史蒂芬·平克：人性与人类进步。*

I want to talk or ask you about the common knowledge and creativity. So common knowledge kind of converges on the expected and on that that is common knowledge. At the same time, we humans prize very highly creativity, which is often a solution to a problem that is not common and that is out of the way. How do the two concepts, how do you reconcile them? What role does creativity play in this perspective?

> 我想问共同知识与创造力的关系。共同知识似乎使人趋向已有预期和已经共有的知识；同时，我们又高度珍视创造力，而创造力通常是给问题找到一种不寻常、另辟蹊径的解法。这两个概念如何协调？在这个框架中，创造力发挥什么作用？

Yeah, I suppose that the connection might be that if it's kind of common knowledge, in this case common belief, common perhaps false belief that some problem is unsolvable or there's only one way to solve it, that can inhibit people from trying to come up with a new solution. This can inhibit creativity and perhaps you need the equivalent of the boy in the emperor's new clothes who is unaware of the social sanction of breaking the, in this case, common misconception. The puzzle in creativity, and I have given some thought to it, is it is not enough that a suggestion be novel or that no one else has thought about it because the vast majority of ideas are wrong. And the puzzle of creativity is not so much how does someone come up with a new idea that no one has ever thought of before, but how does someone come up with a **new GOOD idea** that's worth paying attention to when someone thinks of it. Because often the reason that some new idea is not tried is it's a bad idea.

> 两者可能的联系是：如果某种共同知识——这里更准确地说是共同信念，而且可能是错误信念——认定某个问题无解，或只有一种解法，就会阻止人们尝试新解法，抑制创造力。这时也许需要《皇帝的新装》里小男孩那样的人，不知道打破共同误解会遭受社会惩罚。我思考过创造力的难题：一个建议新颖、从未有人想过，还不够，因为绝大多数想法都是错的。问题不只是怎样产生前所未有的新想法，而是怎样产生值得关注的**新的好想法**。许多新想法从未被尝试，往往正因为它们不好。

So I've given some thought to that. I think that a lot of it, a lot of creative epiphanies are either combinations of ideas that were all sitting isolated that for the first time someone brings together. Because there's a combinatorial explosion of ways in which two ideas can be brought together. So the person who can find the one that perhaps by some equivalent of mental gradient descent find a new combination of parameter settings that gives you an increment of improvement might be one route. Another one might be through analogy. That there are commonalities between superficially different systems that someone notices that they are both examples of the same system and makes the leap from one to another. So for example, Newton, thinking that the relationship between the sun and a planet is like the relationship between a pole and a tether ball. They're centripetal systems where there is linear momentum, there's a centripetal force and the result is circular motion. Or Darwin making the leap from pigeon breeders artificially selecting for some trait and the natural difference between which organisms live and which organisms die being a natural selection.

> 我认为，许多创造性的顿悟，是第一次把原本孤立的想法组合起来。两个想法组合的方式会产生组合爆炸，找到有用组合的人，也许像在心智中进行梯度下降，发现某组新的参数搭配能带来一点改进。这可能是一条途径。另一条是类比：表面不同的系统之间存在共性，有人看出它们属于同一种系统，于是从一个跃迁到另一个。比如牛顿把太阳与行星的关系，类比为杆子与绳系球的关系；二者都是向心系统，具有线性动量与向心力，结果形成圆周运动。又如达尔文，从养鸽人对某种特征的人工选择，跨越到生物存活和死亡的自然差别，把它理解为自然选择。

Even in technological improvements often there has to be some kind of analogy that occurs to the first person as a creative leap. So I once read a story about how the synthetic paintbrush was invented as opposed to using animal bristles. Turns out that plastic bristles resulted in a bad brush. And no one could figure out why until someone realized that a brush is not a paddle that wipes paint on a surface but it's a pump. And that the spaces between the bristles were like pipes and that bending the paintbrush is pumping paint out through those hundreds of microscopic pipes. **NOT** smearing, **BUT** pumping. That analogy which turns out to be apt was a way in which a creative leap led to a technological innovation.

> 技术进步也经常源于第一个人的类比跃迁。我读过合成材料油漆刷的发明故事：不用动物鬃毛，换成塑料刷毛，结果刷子很不好用。没人明白为什么，直到有人认识到，刷子不是把油漆抹到表面的桨片，而是一台泵。刷毛之间的空隙像管道，刷子弯曲时，会把油漆从数百条微小管道中泵出来。**不是涂抹，而是泵送。**这个恰当的类比，让创造性跃迁带来了技术创新。


### 6.4　自动化、工作与工程的困难

> *来源：为什么聪明的人会相信愚蠢的事情：英语原声与逐段中译。*

You've written a bit about AI. This is the latest moral panic. In a broad sense. Should I be worried about this? Because we see these things with wonder. I cannot believe this. It's answering me in these fluid answers in ChatGPT. But oh Lord, this could go horribly wrong, couldn't it? Is that, am I being too pessimistic about that?

> 你写过一些人工智能的文章。这是最新的道德恐慌。从总体上说，我该担心吗？我们惊叹于 ChatGPT 如此流畅的回答，简直难以置信；但天哪，它也可能出大问题。我是不是过分悲观了？

Yeah, I think so. there clearly could be harms with AI as there are harms with any technology. But I don't think that the doomers have made a good case. That is the idea that, that AI will, by some accounts, inevitably try to take over and therefore it will extinguish us the way we did other species, the way we did indigenous peoples.

> 我认为是。人工智能当然可能造成伤害，任何技术都一样。但我不认为末日论者已经提出有力论证：他们认为 AI 会试图接管一切，有些人甚至说这是不可避免的；然后，像人类对待其他物种和原住民那样消灭我们。

It's not a jobs argument. Like this will take- That's separate. That's separate. Okay. that I think, I think we should be so lucky. we'd have to kind of figure out how people can pay for food and rent. But if there's that much abundance, then that strikes me as a kind of a, smaller problem. In fact, it has to be a net huge benefit. People don't have to do boring, dangerous jobs. Instead, stuff gets produced, things happen for free. it's amazing, especially with a population that's likely to decline. But granted, if we don't come up with new policies, whether it's a universal basic income or a negative income tax or finding new uses for human capital, there could be some rough times, but it's not the end of the human species.

> 这不是说工作会被取代的论证吧？那是另一回事。对，另一回事。关于那一点，我倒觉得真能这样就很幸运。我们当然得解决人们怎么买食物、付房租，但如果物质真的极为丰富，那是相对小的问题。总体上应该是巨大的净收益：人不再做无聊危险的工作，物品和服务自动、免费地产生，尤其考虑到人口可能下降，简直令人惊叹。当然，如果没有新政策，比如全民基本收入、负所得税，或为人力资本寻找新用途，可能有艰难时期，但**不是人类物种的终结**。

Are we on the precipice of those times? there seems to be a lot of change coming. There is. I don't think it's going to be that fast. So we still do not have cars that can go from point A to point B and allow you to read a magazine, even though those have been predicted for a long time.

> 我们已经站在那种时代的门口了吗？似乎很多变化就要到来。确实，但我不认为会那么快。人们预测自动驾驶很久了，可我们仍没有能随意从 A 点开到 B 点，同时让你安心读杂志的车。

But the fact that you can't read a magazine and let your Tesla drive you to work shows that these problems can be really hard. The reason that they're hard, even when they work a lot of the time, is that it's not good enough to work a lot of the time. There's the times that they don't work that you worry about that require human common sense, like a construction worker holding up a hand-painted document. Or a construction sign telling you about a detour. You've got to be able to read the sign. Teslas can't read, as far as I know. All those edge cases, as they say, mean that it's really harder than we think to build infallible technologies.

> 但不能一边读杂志、一边让特斯拉送你上班，正说明问题很难。即使多数时候能工作，也不够：你担心的正是它不起作用的那些时刻，那里需要人的常识。例如，施工工人举起手写的绕行指示牌，你得读懂它。据我所知，特斯拉还不会这样阅读。这些所谓边缘情形，说明打造不会出错的技术，比我们想象的困难得多。

Interviewer: Can you give the strongest case that AI will produce harms we are not prepared for? Pinker: It certainly will produce some unanticipated harms, as technologies do. I do not need to strengthen that argument on someone else’s behalf; I accept it. What I question is the argument for doom.

> 主持人：你能否给出最有力的论证，说明 AI 会带来我们尚未准备好的伤害？平克：它当然会带来某些未预料到的伤害，技术一直如此。我不必替别人强化这项论证，因为我直接承认它。真正有疑问的是末日论的论证。


### 6.5　智能是否意味着支配欲

> *来源：为什么聪明的人会相信愚蠢的事情：英语原声与逐段中译。*

There are two versions of the argument that AI could bring about humanity’s doom. The first draws an analogy: humans dominated other species, sometimes driving them to extinction, and dominated many Indigenous peoples; AI might treat Homo sapiens as we treated the passenger pigeon. The second concerns goals that are not aligned with human values. A hypothetical AI told to eliminate cancer might eliminate every human, thereby making cancer disappear. In pursuing its goal, it would kill us as collateral damage. These are arguments that intelligent people seriously put forward.

> 关于 AI 可能导致人类毁灭的论证，有两个版本。第一种作了一个类比：人类支配其他物种，有时使它们灭绝，也支配许多原住民；AI 可能像人类对待旅鸽那样对待智人。第二种涉及与人类价值不一致的目标。假设一个 AI 被要求消灭癌症，它可能通过消灭所有人来让癌症消失；在追求目标的过程中，人类成为附带伤害。这些是一些聪明人认真提出的论证。

Or that you give an AI a goal of regulating the water level behind a dam, and it floods a city because that's one way to keep the water level as specified. That could happen now. That could happen now, yeah. Technology can screw up quite a bit. you see what happened with the air buses and things like this. this is a common thing, right? Yeah. I would imagine it would be more precise.

> 或者，你让 AI 调节水坝后的水位，它却淹了一座城，因为这也是维持指定水位的办法。这现在就可能发生。对，现在也可能，技术会出很大的错。看看空客之类发生的事情，技术出问题并不稀奇。我想 AI 会更精确些。

Well, it would be more precise. to extend the argument, the singularity could happen where AI would recursively improve its own intelligence. It would be so smart that among the things that it could do is figure out how to get smarter, which would make it figure out how to get still smarter, and that would include disabling all human efforts to disable it. It could include brainwashing or bribing or manipulating humans to do its bidding when it has not been empowered by being connected to the grid or the net or actual machinery. And so it could recruit armies of people to pursue its goals, and those goals may come at the expense of human well-being.

> 它会更精确。继续沿着那个论证说，可能出现奇点：AI 递归地提升自己的智能，聪明到能想出如何更聪明，再想出怎样进一步更聪明，包括阻止人类将它关闭。即使没有直接接入电网、互联网或实际机器，它也可能通过洗脑、贿赂、操纵，让人替它做事，招募大批人实现自己的目标，而那些目标可能牺牲人类福祉。

this is dystopian science fiction, but is that possible? Could that ever happen? Well, the thing is, if you think that it's coherent, that the singularity is coherent, that recursively improving one's own intelligence is coherent, then maybe. I think it's not coherent because I think it is extrapolating from smarter to infallible and omniscient.

> 这听起来像反乌托邦科幻，但可能发生吗？如果你认为奇点、递归提升自身智能这些概念是成立的，那也许可能。我不认为这套论证自洽，因为它从“更聪明”推到了“绝对不会犯错、无所不知”。

I think there are a number of things wrong with these arguments. One of them is that just because you're smarter does **NOT NECESSARILY** mean that you have an urge to dominate. In Homo sapiens, intelligence comes bundled with aggression and dominance and all the other nasty traits that we came saddled with because we're the product of a competitive process, natural selection. But if something is designed, there's no reason that it should want anything. It wants what we tell it to want. That wanting something and being smart enough to know how to get it are two completely different things. It's not the case that like in myths like the golem and the sorcerer's apprentice and so on, that as you get smarter, that kind of stokes a thirst for power, for dominance. I think it's a human projection.

> 这些论证有几个问题。首先，**更聪明并不必然意味着更想支配别人**。在人类身上，智能与攻击性、支配欲等不良特质捆绑，是因为我们来自自然选择这个竞争过程。但设计出来的系统，并没有理由天然想要什么；它想要的是我们让它想要的东西。想要某物，与足够聪明而知道怎样得到它，是完全不同的两回事。不是像魔像、魔法师学徒那些故事一样，越聪明就越渴望权力和支配。我认为，那是人的投射。

As I kind of put it, we know that there are creatures that are capable of advanced intelligence without the urge to dominate and conquer. They're called women. So that's just one example. Or those old enough to remember Al Capp's Shmoos, the cartoon figures who had extraordinary powers but they were also extraordinarily altruistic. So they would barbecue themselves for the pleasure of human diners. They would just bring about a utopia. That is no more or less plausible than an AI conquistador or megalomaniacs. That's one thing.

> 我有时这样说：我们知道，有些生物拥有很高的智能，却没有支配和征服的冲动，她们叫女性。这只是个例子。年长一些的人或许还记得 Al Capp 笔下的 Shmoo：能力非凡，又极其利他，甚至会把自己烤好，让人类食客享用，简直能带来乌托邦。这样的 AI，与征服者或权力狂式的 AI，相比并没有更合理或更不合理。

Another is that the scenarios of giving a system a single goal and it not occurring to you that there could be side effects is just so patently idiotic that I'm just not worried about people accidentally doing it. So that's the thing. The idea is that the system consists of carrying out multiple conflicting goals. You've got a car with an engine that makes it go as fast as possible and you also put in brakes and a steering wheel and a catalytic converter and all the rest. That's what engineering is.

> 另一个问题是：给系统一个单一目标，却完全没想到副作用，这种情境实在蠢得明显，我不担心人们会无意中这么做。工程本来就是协调多个相互冲突的目标。汽车有让它尽量跑快的发动机，也有刹车、方向盘、催化转换器等，这才是工程。

A system that figures out that one way of eliminating cancer is eliminating humans—that's **NOT** artificial intelligence, that's artificial stupidity. Because bringing about multiple simultaneous goals is what intelligence is. If you single-mindedly pursue one goal at the expense of everything else, that is idiocy. It's not intelligence. It's double idiocy because the system would be idiotic and any engineer who would build a system like that would be more idiotic. It requires human input. Yeah. You're giving it the goal. Yeah, exactly. Who would be moronic enough to both empower and direct an untested system? I can think of a few people, but I don't think they're scientists. Here's AI safety. Don't let them have access to the grid.

> 一个系统若聪明到想到“消灭人类就能消灭癌症”，那**不是**人工智能，而是人工愚蠢。智能意味着同时实现多个目标。如果不顾一切只追一个目标，那是愚蠢，不是智能。还是双重愚蠢：系统愚蠢，造出它的工程师更愚蠢。它毕竟需要人的输入，是人给它目标。对。谁会蠢到既给一个未经测试的系统巨大能力，又让它这样行动？我能想到几个人，但他们不是科学家。那就谈 AI 安全：别让他们接入电网。


### 6.6　安全文化与风险感知

> *来源：Lex Fridman 访谈史蒂芬·平克：恐惧由想象力驱动，而非数据（2018 年 10 月）。*

The code of engineering is: you **DO NOT** implement a system with massive control before testing it. Now, perhaps the culture of engineering will radically change, then I would worry, but I don't see any signs that engineers will suddenly do idiotic things like put an electrical power plant under the control of a system that they haven't tested first, or all of these scenarios not only imagine a, almost a magically powered intelligence, including things like cure cancer, which is probably an incoherent goal because there's so many different kinds of cancer or bring about world peace. how do you even specify that as a goal? But the scenarios also imagine some degree of control of every molecule in the universe, which not only is itself unlikely, but we would not start to connect these systems to infrastructure without testing as we would any kind of engineering system. Now, maybe some engineers will be irresponsible. We need legal and regulatory responsibility implemented so that engineers don't do things that are stupid by their own standards. But I’ve never seen enough of a plausible scenario of existential threat to devote large amounts of brainpower to forestall it.

> 工程准则是：**不能在未经测试之前，就部署一个具有巨大控制权的系统。**也许工程文化会发生彻底改变，那样我会担忧；但我看不到任何迹象表明，工程师会突然做出愚蠢的事情，比如让未经测试的系统控制发电厂。这些情境不只设想了一种近乎魔法般强大的智能，能“治愈癌症”——癌症有那么多种，这个笼统目标本身可能就不自洽——或实现“世界和平”，甚至不知道如何精确定义这样的目标；还设想它在某种程度上能够控制宇宙中的每个分子。这不仅本身不大可能，我们也不会不经测试就把系统接入基础设施，任何工程系统都应经过测试。当然，某些工程师可能不负责任，因此需要法律、监管和责任制度，防止他们做出按自身标准也愚蠢的事。但我从未看到足够可信的生存性威胁情境，值得为预防它投入大量脑力。

Interviewer: Do you believe that engineering, reason, and science can guide new technology toward safety?

> 主持人：你相信工程、理性和科学能够引导新技术，使它更安全吗？

If the same, granted the same culture of safety that currently is part of the engineering mindset for airplanes, for example. So, yeah, I don't think that should be thrown out the window and that untested all powerful systems should be suddenly implemented. But there's no reason to think they are. And in fact, if you look at the progress of artificial intelligence, it's been, it's been impressive, especially in the last ten years or so. But the idea that suddenly there'll be a step function that all of a sudden before we know it will be all powerful, that there'll be some kind of recursive self-improvement, some kind of FOOM[^lex-foom] is also fanciful. Certainly, the technology that now impresses us, such as deep learning, where you train something on hundreds of thousands or millions of examples. They're not hundreds of thousands of problems of which curing cancer is a typical example. And so the kind of techniques that have allowed AI to increase in the last five years are not the kind that are going to lead to this fantasy of exponential sudden self-improvement. So it's I think it's kind of a magical thinking. It’s **NOT** based on our understanding of how AI actually works.

> 前提是延续现有的安全文化，例如飞机工程中的安全思维。当然，我不认为应该把它抛到脑后，突然部署一个未经测试、无所不能的系统，但也没有理由认为会这样做。事实上，看看人工智能的进展，尤其过去十年左右，确实令人印象深刻。但认为它会突然阶跃，在我们还未反应过来时就无所不能，出现递归式自我改进或某种“FOOM”式智能爆发，同样是幻想。至少现在让我们印象深刻的技术，例如深度学习，是用数十万或数百万个例子训练系统；我们并没有数十万个以治愈癌症为典型例子的问题。因此，过去五年推动人工智能进步的技术，并不是会导致这种突然指数式自我改进幻想的技术。我认为这是一种魔法思维，**不是**根据人工智能实际如何运作而作出的判断。

Now, give me a chance here. So you said fanciful, magical thinking. In his TED talk, Sam Harris says that thinking about AI killing all human civilization is somehow fun intellectually. Now, I have to say, as a scientist engineer, I don't find it fun. But when I'm having beer with my non-AI friends, there is indeed something fun and appealing about it, like talking about an episode of Black Mirror, considering if a large meteor is headed towards Earth. We were just told that a large meteor is headed towards Earth, something like this. And can you relate to this sense of fun? And do you understand the psychology of it?

> 那让我从另一个角度问。你用了“幻想”和“魔法思维”这些词。萨姆·哈里斯在 TED 演讲中说，思考人工智能毁灭整个人类文明，在智力上似乎颇有趣。我必须说，作为科学家和工程师，我不觉得有趣。但与不研究人工智能的朋友喝啤酒时，这个话题确实有某种乐趣和吸引力，像讨论一集《黑镜》，或者设想刚得知一颗巨大陨石正飞向地球。你能理解这种乐趣吗？能理解背后的心理机制吗？

That's a good question. I personally don't find it fun. I find it kind of actually a waste of time because there are genuine threats that we ought to be thinking about, like pandemics, like cybersecurity vulnerabilities, like the possibility of nuclear war. And certainly climate change. This is enough to fill many conversations. And I think Sam did put his finger on something, namely that there is a community, sometimes called the rationality community, that delights in using its brainpower to come up with scenarios that would not occur to mere mortals, to less cerebral people. So there is a kind of intellectual thrill in finding new things to worry about that no one has worried about yet.

> 这是个好问题。我个人不觉得有趣，反而觉得有点浪费时间，因为有真正的威胁需要思考，例如大流行病、网络安全漏洞、核战争的可能性，当然还有气候变化。这些已经足够谈上很久。不过，萨姆确实指出了一件事：有一个有时被称为“理性社群”的群体，乐于运用脑力构想普通人、不那么醉心智力活动的人想不到的情境。因此，发现别人还没有担心过的新风险，确实会带来一种智力上的兴奋。

I actually think, though, that not only is it a kind of fun that doesn't give me particular pleasure, but I think there can be a pernicious side to it, namely that you overcome people with such dread, such fatalism, that there's so many ways to die, to annihilate our civilization, that we may as well enjoy life while we can. There's nothing we can do about it. If climate change doesn't do us in, then runaway robots will. So let's enjoy ourselves now. We've got to prioritize. We have to look at threats that are close to certainty, such as climate change, and distinguish those from ones that are merely imaginable, but with infinitesimal probabilities. And we have to take into account people's worry budget. You can't worry about everything. And if you sow dread and fear and terror, you can't do anything about it. If you sow fear and terror and fatalism, it can lead to a kind of numbness. Well, these problems are overwhelming, and the engineers are just going to kill us all. So let's either destroy the entire infrastructure of science, technology, or let's just enjoy life while we can.

> 但这种乐趣不只让我感受不到多大愉悦，我还认为它可能有害：它会让人充满恐惧和宿命感，觉得死亡、文明覆灭的途径这么多，不如趁现在享受人生，反正无能为力。如果气候变化没毁掉我们，失控的机器人也会。所以，我们必须分清轻重缓急，把气候变化这种近乎确定的威胁，与仅仅可以想象、概率却极低的情境区分开。还要考虑人的“担忧预算”，不可能担心所有事情。不断播撒恐惧、惊骇和宿命感，会导致麻木：“这些问题太庞大，工程师反正会把我们全杀掉。那就摧毁整个科学技术基础设施，或者趁还能享受时及时行乐吧。”

So there's a certain line of worry, which I'm worried about a lot of things in engineering. There's a certain line of worry when you cross, you're allowed to cross, that it becomes paralyzed fear as opposed to productive fear. And that's kind of what you're highlighting. Exactly right.

> 所以，担忧存在某条界线。我在工程领域也担心很多事情，但越过某个界限，担忧就会变成令人瘫痪的恐惧，而不是有建设性的恐惧。你强调的是这一点。——完全正确。

We know that human effort is not well calibrated against risk, because a basic tenet of cognitive psychology is that **perception of risk, and hence perception of fear, is driven by imaginability, NOT by data.** And so we misallocate vast amounts of resources to that. Right. And that leads us to avoiding terrorism, which kills on average about six Americans a year, with one exception of 9-11. We invade countries. We invent entire new departments of government with massive expenditure of resources and lives to defend ourselves against a trivial risk. Whereas guaranteed risks, and you mentioned, as one of them, you mentioned traffic fatalities, and even risks that are not here, but are plausible enough to worry about, like pandemics, like nuclear war, receive far too little attention. In presidential debates, there's no discussion of how to minimize the risk of nuclear war. Lots of discussion of terrorism, for example. And so I think it’s essential to calibrate our budget of fear, worry, concern, planning to the actual probability of harm.

> 我们知道，人类投入的努力与实际风险并没有很好地匹配。认知心理学的一条基本认识是：**风险感知，以及由此产生的恐惧，受可想象性驱动，而不是受数据驱动。**因此，我们把大量资源用错了地方。例如，除“9·11”这一例外，恐怖主义平均每年造成约六名美国人死亡；为了避免这种风险，我们入侵其他国家，成立全新的政府部门，耗费大量资源与生命去防御一个很小的风险。相比之下，确定存在的风险，例如你提到的交通死亡，甚至尚未发生、但足够可信而值得担忧的风险，例如大流行病和核战争，受到的关注都太少。总统辩论很少讨论如何降低核战争风险，却大量谈论恐怖主义。因此，我认为，必须依据实际伤害发生的概率，校准我们在恐惧、担忧、关切和规划上的投入。


### 6.7　已经发生的改善，与尚未消除的风险

> *来源：为什么聪明的人会相信愚蠢的事情：英语原声与逐段中译。*

Interviewer: Even if AI does not destroy us, many people argue that we may destroy ourselves through nuclear war or environmental disaster. Should we be worrying about ourselves rather than robots?

> 主持人：即使 AI 不会毁灭我们，仍有许多人认为，我们可能通过核战争或环境灾难毁灭自己。与其担心机器人，我们是否更该担心自己？

Well, I think worrying about ourselves should be a higher priority, like making nuclear weapons harder to deploy accidentally, making leaders less empowered to wield the nuclear arsenal capriciously or impulsively. I think those are very worthy goals. I think an even longer-term worthy goal is getting rid of them.

> 我认为，担心我们自己确实应有更高优先级，比如让核武器更难被意外使用，让领导人更难随意或冲动地动用核武库。这些都是值得追求的目标。更长远、同样值得追求的目标，是彻底消除核武器。

This was an argument against your book which caused a lot of controversy, The Better Angels of Our Nature, which basically says wars are less frequent, less deadly than they've been at any time, despite the fact that you think otherwise. Some of the counterarguments, one of which is just that. Yeah, might be fewer of them, but we have nuclear weapons everywhere, and we could have one and destroy everything in one go. What do you make of that argument?

> 这也是对你那本引发很多争论的《人性中的善良天使》的反驳之一。书中大意是：尽管人们觉得不是这样，战争其实比过去更少、致命性更低。反对者说，战争也许更少了，但核武器到处都是，一场就能毁掉一切。你怎么看？

Well, that is possible. In that sense, that's a new risk that we didn't have more than 75 years ago, and it's a risk that we should do something about. Yeah, but you don't think that sort of invalidates the argument in any way? Well, no, it's a different argument. That is, the wars that do happen, there's a war that might happen, and the wars that do happen, happen less often and are less deadly. The war that could happen could be worse. So do you want to combine those into one statement? I think it's kind of apples and oranges. They're both true, and we've got to figure out how to make sense of both of them.

> 那确实可能。从这个意义说，这是七十五年前没有的新风险，我们应该采取行动。但这不会使原来的论点失效吗？不会，这是另一个论点。**已经发生的战争，与可能发生的战争，是两件事。**实际发生的战争更少、死亡更少；未来可能发生的战争却可能更糟。要把它们合成一句话吗？我觉得有点像混比苹果和橙子。两者都真实，我们得理解如何同时看待它们。

> *来源：史蒂芬·平克：人性与人类进步。*

When asked to forecast the next fifty to a hundred years, I have to be humble, being familiar with the literature on forecasting. Again, I hate to keep dumping on journalism, but if you look at predictions made by pundits and columnists, they’re basically random. If you go back five years, see what the experts predicted and what actually happened, they’re throwing darts blindfolded. But there are what are called superforecasters who have a positive track record of predicting events in the future, basically using Bayesian methods. They look up the background rates, the base rates. They specifically avoid cognitive biases like overconfidence, like availability, and they do a Bayesian estimate. Will the price of petroleum exceed a certain level in the next twelve months? Will there be a major terrorist attack on a European city in the next twenty-four months? Hard and fast predictions like that. The superforecasters are pretty good, much better than experts who are not superforecasters. However, their rate of accuracy falls to chance after five years. So beyond five years, basically no one can predict the future with any kind of precision, which is why I would be very hesitant to predict it for fifty to a hundred years.

> 如果要预测未来五十到一百年，了解预测研究之后，我必须谦虚一点。我不想总批评新闻业，但看看评论家、专栏作家的预测，基本和随机猜测差不多。回顾五年前专家说了什么，再看实际发生什么，就像蒙着眼扔飞镖。不过，确实有“超级预测者”，对未来事件有较好的预测记录，主要使用贝叶斯方法：查询背景发生率、基准率，有意识地避开过度自信、可得性等认知偏差，再作贝叶斯估计。例如未来十二个月油价是否超过某个水平，未来二十四个月欧洲城市是否会发生重大恐怖袭击。他们很擅长这种明确、可检验的预测，比其他专家强得多。然而，超过五年之后，他们的准确率就降至随机水平。因此，五年以上基本没人能精确预测未来，这就是我很不愿预测五十至一百年的原因。

Even though I have denied that the case for progress is a case for optimism, and what I emphasize is just looking at data rather than headlines, I will confess that I am something of an optimist, based just on the discovery that there has been so much progress. Our ancestors saw problems. They tried to solve them. If they kept what worked, if they did not repeat their mistakes, then gradually progress happened. It seems that our background assumption should be that progress will continue to happen, that problems are solvable. I like to quote the physicist David Deutsch, a pioneer in quantum computing, who said that unless something is ruled out by the laws of nature, all problems are solvable given the right knowledge. That means progress is possible. Looking back suggests that progress is not just a theoretical possibility, but it has happened.

> 虽然我否认讨论进步就是为乐观主义辩护，强调的只是看数据而不看标题，但我承认，发现人类已取得如此多进步，的确使我有些乐观。祖先发现问题，尝试解决；如果保留有效办法，不重犯错误，进步就会逐步发生。因此，我们似乎应该把“进步可以继续、问题可以解决”作为背景假设。我喜欢引用量子计算先驱、物理学家戴维·多伊奇的话：除非自然定律禁止，否则只要具备恰当知识，所有问题都可以解决。这意味着进步是可能的。回顾历史可见，它不只是理论可能，而是已经发生过。

Certainly in the case of AI, I think it’s easy to fool yourself into thinking that AI is a kind of magic, a kind of omnipotence that can just solve any problem. Predictions that we’re going to cure cancer in five years, I think, are ridiculous, simply because **learning about the world has a speed limit determined by the world**. You have to do experiments. You’ve got to gather data. You’ve got to see what happens when you apply treatments, and whether the tumor shrinks or not. Unless AI is omniscient about every molecule in the universe, like Laplace’s famous demon, a lot of knowledge is going to be limited by the rate of physical processes in the world, and by the fact that we will never have data on the position and velocity of every molecule in the universe. So there are going to be built-in limits. Nonetheless, I think there are sober predictions about what AI could do: automate dangerous and boring tasks, accelerate discovery when there are combinatorially explosive spaces. People in this audience know them far better than me.

> 谈人工智能时，人很容易骗自己，以为它是一种魔法、无所不能，可以解决任何问题。我认为“未来五年治愈癌症”之类的预测很荒唐，因为**了解世界的速度上限，由世界本身决定**。必须做实验、收集数据、观察治疗后发生什么，肿瘤是否缩小。除非人工智能像著名的“拉普拉斯妖”那样，知道宇宙中每个分子的全部信息，否则大量知识仍受限于现实物理过程的速度，也受限于我们永远不可能掌握宇宙中所有分子的位置和速度数据。这些是内在限制。不过，关于人工智能的作用，也有冷静合理的预测：自动完成危险、无聊的任务，在具有组合爆炸的搜索空间里加速发现。在座各位对此比我了解得多。

I’ll add one asterisk, though. This, I’m sure, has occurred to many of you, and has of course occurred to the people who are worried about AI safety: the expectation based on the past may not be a good guide to tail risks, to things that are extremely improbable but potentially catastrophic, as in nuclear war in the case of international relations. There’s a whole industry of people thinking up what I consider to be rather preposterous scenarios of how AI could go rogue, could seek to take over, could treat us the way we treat animals, could use us as raw materials to pursue some goal. I tend to think that those are highly unlikely, but of course the probability is **NOT ZERO**, so we ought to think about preventing them from happening.

> 但还要加一个注脚，我相信很多人、尤其关心人工智能安全的人都想到了：根据过去形成的预期，未必能很好地指导我们应对尾部风险，也就是概率极低但可能灾难性的事件，例如国际关系中的核战争。现在有一大批人专门设想在我看来相当离奇的情景：人工智能失控、寻求接管、像我们对待动物一样对待我们，或者把人类当作原料去实现某个目标。我倾向于认为这些情况极不可能，**但概率当然不是零，所以我们应当思考如何防止它们发生。**


<a id="chapter-7"></a>

## 尾声　知识、生活与继续改善世界的理由

### 生命的意义与知识的价值

> *来源：Lex Fridman 访谈史蒂芬·平克：恐惧由想象力驱动，而非数据（2018 年 10 月）。*

What is the meaning of life? Is it A, to attain knowledge, as Plato said, B, to attain power, as Nietzsche said, C, to escape death, as Ernest Becker said, D, to propagate our genes, as Darwin and others have said, E, there is no meaning, as the nihilists have said, F, knowing the meaning of life is beyond our cognitive capabilities, as Steven Pinker said, based on my interpretation 20 years ago, and G, none of the above.

> 生命的意义是什么？A，像柏拉图所说的那样，获取知识；B，像尼采所说的那样，获得权力；C，像欧内斯特·贝克尔所说的那样，逃避死亡；D，像达尔文等人所说的那样，传播我们的基因；E，像虚无主义者所说的那样，生命没有意义；F，生命的意义超出了我们的认知能力——这是我对史蒂芬·平克二十年前观点的理解；还是 G，以上都不是？

I'd say A comes closest, but I would amend that to attaining not only knowledge, but fulfillment more generally. That is, life, health, stimulation, access to the living, cultural, and social world. Now, this is **our meaning of life**. It's not the meaning of life, if you were to ask our genes. Their meaning is to propagate copies of themselves, but that is distinct from the meaning that the brain that they lead to sets for itself.

> 我会说 A 最接近，但我想把它修改为：不仅获取知识，还要获得更广泛的充实感，也就是生命、健康、刺激，以及接触生物世界、文化世界和社会世界的机会。不过，这是**我们自己的生命意义**。如果你去问基因，答案并不相同：基因的意义是复制自身。但基因产生的大脑为自己设定的意义，与基因自身的意义是不同的。

So, to you, is knowledge a small subset or a large subset?

> 所以对你来说，知识只是其中很小的一部分，还是很大的一部分？

It's a large subset, but it's not the entirety of human striving, because we also want to interact with people, we want to experience beauty, we want to experience the richness of the natural world, but understanding what makes the universe tick is way up there. For some of us more than others, certainly for me, that's one of the top five.

> 很大的一部分，但不是人类全部追求。我们还想与人交往，体验美，感受自然世界的丰富。不过，理解宇宙如何运转，确实排在很重要的位置。有些人比另一些人更看重它；至少对我来说，它是最重要的五项追求之一。

Is that a fundamental aspect? Are you just describing your own preference, or is this a fundamental aspect of human nature, to seek knowledge? In your latest book, you talk about the power, the usefulness of rationality and reason and so on. Is that a fundamental nature of human beings, or is it something we should just strive for?

> 这是人性的一个根本特征吗？你只是在描述个人偏好，还是说追求知识本身就是人性的根本方面？你在新书中谈到理性、推理的力量与用途。那么，这是人类固有的本性，还是我们应当努力追求的东西？

It's both. We’re capable of striving for it, because it is one of the things that make us what we are, Homo sapiens. Wise men. We are unusual among animals in the degree to which we acquire knowledge and use it to survive. We make tools, we strike agreements via language, we extract poisons, we predict the behavior of animals, we try to get at the workings of plants. And when I say we, I don't just mean we in the modern West, but we as a species everywhere, which is how we've managed to occupy a niche on the planet, how we've managed to drive other animals to extinction, and the refinement of reason in pursuit of human well-being, of health, happiness, social richness, cultural richness, is our main challenge in the present. That is, using our intellect, using our knowledge to figure out how the world works, how we work, in order to make discoveries and strike agreements that make us all better off in the long run.

> 两者都是。我们能够追求它，是因为它本来就是使我们成为智人——“有智慧的人”——的特征之一。与其他动物相比，我们获取知识并用知识生存的能力很不寻常。我们制造工具，通过语言达成协议，提取毒物，预测动物行为，努力理解植物如何运作。我说的“我们”，不只是现代西方人，而是各地的人类。正因为如此，我们才能占据地球上的生态位，甚至使其他动物灭绝。当前的主要挑战，是完善理性，以追求人类福祉，包括健康、幸福、丰富的社会生活和文化生活。也就是用我们的智慧和知识，理解世界和自身如何运作，作出发现，达成协议，让所有人长远来看都过得更好。


### 尚未完成的人类故事

> *来源：这个世界在变好吗？。*

We are born into a pitiless universe, facing steep odds against life-enabling order and in constant jeopardy of falling apart. We were shaped by a process that is ruthlessly competitive. We are made from crooked timber, vulnerable to illusions, self-centeredness, and, at times, astounding stupidity.

> 我们出生在一个冷酷无情的宇宙中，让生命得以存在的秩序来之不易，并且时刻面临瓦解的危险。我们由一个竞争残酷的过程塑造而成。我们由弯曲的木料造就，容易陷入幻觉、自我中心，有时还会愚蠢得令人震惊。

Yet human nature has also been blessed with resources that open a space for a kind of redemption. We are endowed with the power to combine ideas recursively, to have thoughts about our thoughts. We have an instinct for language, allowing us to share the fruits of our ingenuity and experience. We are endowed with a capacity for sympathy, for pity, imagination, compassion, commiseration.

> 然而，人性也拥有一些禀赋，为某种自我救赎留下了空间。我们能够递归地组合观念，能够思考自己的思想。我们拥有语言本能，可以分享才智与经验的成果。我们还拥有同情、怜悯、想象、慈悲以及与他人共感的能力。

These endowments have found ways to magnify their own power. The scope of language has been augmented by the written, printed, and electronic word. Our circle of sympathy has been expanded by history, journalism, and the narrative arts. And our puny rational faculties have been multiplied by the norms and institutions of reason: intellectual curiosity, open debate, skepticism of authority and dogma, and the burden of proof to verify ideas by confronting them against reality.

> 这些禀赋已经找到了放大自身力量的方法。文字、印刷和电子文本拓展了语言的影响范围；历史、新闻和叙事艺术扩展了我们的同情圈；理性的规范与制度则成倍放大了我们有限的理性能力：求知欲、公开辩论、对权威和教条的怀疑，以及让观念接受现实检验的举证责任。

As the spiral of recursive improvement gathers momentum, we eke out victories against the forces that grind us down, not least the darker parts of our own nature. We penetrate the mysteries of the cosmos, including life and mind. We live longer, suffer less, learn more, get smarter, and enjoy more small pleasures and rich experiences. Fewer of us are killed, assaulted, enslaved, exploited, or oppressed by others. From a few oases, the territories with peace and prosperity are growing and could someday encompass the globe.

> 随着这种不断自我改善的循环积聚动力，我们一点点战胜那些摧残我们的力量，其中也包括自身人性中的阴暗面。我们探索宇宙的奥秘，包括生命和心智。我们活得更长，受苦更少，学得更多，变得更聪明，享受更多细小的快乐和丰富的体验。被他人杀害、攻击、奴役、剥削或压迫的人越来越少。和平与繁荣的区域，从最初的几个绿洲不断扩大，有一天可能覆盖全球。

Much suffering remains, and tremendous peril. But ideas on how to reduce them have been voiced, and an infinite number of others are yet to be conceived. *We will never have a perfect world, and it would be dangerous to seek one. But there is no limit to the betterments we can attain if we continue to apply knowledge to enhance human flourishing.*

> 大量痛苦和巨大危险依然存在。但减少它们的想法已经提出，还有无数新的想法等待诞生。*我们永远不会拥有一个完美世界，追求那样的世界也很危险。但只要继续运用知识来增进人类福祉，我们能够实现的改善就没有上限。*

This heroic story is **NOT** just another myth. Myths are fictions, but this one is true—true to the best of our knowledge, which is the only truth we can have. As we learn more, we can show which parts of the story continue to be true and which ones false, as any of them might be and any could become. And this story belongs **NOT** to any tribe, **BUT** to all of humanity, to any sentient creature with the power of reason and the urge to persist in its being. For it requires only the convictions that life is better than death, health is better than sickness, abundance is better than want, freedom is better than coercion, happiness is better than suffering, and knowledge is better than ignorance and superstition.

> 这个英雄般的故事**不只是**又一个神话。神话是虚构的，但这个故事是真实的——按照我们目前最充分的知识，它是真实的，而这也是我们唯一能够拥有的真理。随着了解加深，我们能够判断故事的哪些部分仍然成立，哪些部分是错的，因为任何部分都可能有误，也可能不再成立。这个故事**不属于**任何一个部落，**而属于**全人类，属于任何拥有理性、渴望继续存在的有感知生命。它只要求我们相信：生命比死亡好，健康比疾病好，富足比匮乏好，自由比强制好，幸福比痛苦好，知识比无知与迷信好。

> 重复材料的取舍：

- 《人性与白板》承担人性与遗传的主论证；其他访谈中重复的白板论概述不再收入。
- 《语言学：了解大脑的窗口》解释语言机制；《语言和思想》补充概念隐喻与间接言语，《人性与人类进步》补充共同知识。
- 《终极理性指南》承担概率、贝叶斯推理与求真制度的主论证；Honestly 访谈补充合取谬误、本方偏见与信息可信度。
- 《写作意识的风格》承担写作原理与用法的主论证；AI 时代写作访谈补充概括与例子、声音与幽默，以及大语言模型。
- 进步的长期趋势主要采用《人性与人类进步》中同时讨论改善与倒退的完整论述；《这个世界在变好吗？》补充安全、闲暇和人文主义。
- 人工智能风险以 Honestly 访谈的较完整论证为主，Lex Fridman 访谈补充意识问题与工程安全。相同的回形针、单一目标及支配欲论证不再逐篇重复。
- [比尔·盖茨与史蒂芬·平克谈《当下的启蒙》](4.%E6%AF%94%E5%B0%94%C2%B7%E7%9B%96%E8%8C%A8%E4%B8%8E%E5%8F%B2%E8%92%82%E8%8A%AC%C2%B7%E5%B9%B3%E5%85%8B%E8%B0%88%E3%80%8A%E5%BD%93%E4%B8%8B%E7%9A%84%E5%90%AF%E8%92%99%E3%80%8B%28Av68215840%2CP4%29.md)的核心观点已由更完整的演讲覆盖，未再次收入正文。
- “为什么聪明的人会相信愚蠢的事情”的中文配音与英语原声为同一访谈，采用英语原声的中英对照整理稿。
- 仅为重复既有论点服务的部分时事例子、书单和个人经历未重复收入；与论证直接相关的实例及必要限定保留。

## 注释

[^blank-2]: 这里保留讲者对当时双胞胎和收养研究的解释。其关于家庭影响的强断言不等于所有养育方式、所有环境对孩子都没有影响。

[^ling-holded]: holded、teared、sticked 是用于说明儿童过度规则化的故意错误，不能改成规范过去式。相关例句见[平克的语言习得论文](https://stevenpinker.com/files/pinker/files/jcl_macwhinney_commentary.pdf)。

[^ling-anatomy]: 声带是含肌组织的黏膜褶皱，见[美国 NIDCD 术语说明](https://www.nidcd.nih.gov/glossary/vocal-cords-vocal-folds)。有关喉位下降及演化权衡的解释仍按讲者观点保留。

[^ling-era]: 这段演讲标为 2012 年；语音识别与机器翻译的比较属于当时的技术背景，见 [Big Think 演讲回顾](https://bigthink.com/neuropsych/pinker-linguistics-human-brain/)。

[^progress-sweden]: 瑞典改为右侧通行的时间已据[斯德哥尔摩市史料库](https://stockholmskallan.stockholm.se/teman/stockholmshandelser/hogertrafikomlaggningen-1967/)订正为 1967 年 9 月 3 日 05:00。

[^progress-pluralistic]: “没有男性”“没有人”等是讲者在举例时的概括性说法，不是对沙特男性观点分布的精确统计。

[^reason-roulette]: 这里采用红、黑各占 50% 的简化模型，说明独立事件没有记忆；实际轮盘还可能包含绿色的 0 等格子。

[^honestly-2]: 合取概率不大于任一单项概率，相等也是可能的。这里的连乘指条件概率连乘，并不假定所有细节彼此独立。

[^honestly-3]: “两个月前”与“约 75%”属于访谈中的表述，事件间隔与调查数字未在本次编集中独立核验。

[^reason-quotation]: 此处是讲者对特朗普言论的概括，不是特朗普发言的逐字引文。

[^aiwrite-etymology]: 此处保留受访者对 adultery 与 adulterate 的词义联系的讲解，不将其视为完整的词源考证。

[^progress-literacy]: 此处识字率的时期与统计人群，在现有材料中无法确定；尤其“超过 90%”不能直接等同于全球所有年龄人口的识字率。数字保留为演讲中的表述。

[^progress-suicide]: 这里的 40% 指相对降幅，而非下降四十个百分点；起始年份与具体统计口径仍以讲者所引资料为准，现有材料不足以独立核对。

[^lex-foom]: FOOM 是有关人工智能通过递归自我改进而迅速爆发的情景名称。此处保留讲者对该情景的批评。
